FILE: ./05_Networking_and_Cybersecurity/02_Networking_Basics/02_Networking_Basics.md
════════════════════════════════════════
# Networking 

### Key Topics and Notes
- [Fundamentals of Networking MOC](./00_MOCs/01_Fundamentals_of_Networking_MOC.md)
- [Application Layer Protocols MOC](./00_MOCs/02_Application_Layer_Protocols_MOC.md)
- [Transport Layer Mechanisms MOC](./00_MOCs/03_Transport_Layer_Mechanisms_MOC.md)
- [Network Layer and Routing MOC](./00_MOCs/04_Network_Layer_and_Routing_MOC.md)
- [Link Layer and Local Area Networks MOC](./00_MOCs/05_Link_Layer_and_Local_Area_Networks_MOC.md)
- [Wireless and Mobile Networking MOC](./00_MOCs/06_Wireless_and_Mobile_Networking_MOC.md)
- [Security in Computer Networks MOC](./00_MOCs/07_Security_in_Computer_Networks_MOC.md)
- [Multimedia Networking MOC](./00_MOCs/08_Multimedia_Networking_MOC.md)
- [Network Management MOC](./00_MOCs/09_Network_Management_MOC.md)
- [Emerging Trends and Technologies MOC](./00_MOCs/10_Emerging_Trends_and_Technologies_MOC.md)


## Resources  
- [Ebook: Computer Networking: A Top-Down Approach, James](./01_Networking_a_Top_Down_Approach_James.pdf)  
- [Ebook: Data and Computer Communications by William Stallings](./01_Resources/02_Data_and_Computer_Communications_Williams.pdf)  
- [Practice Problems](./02_Practice-Problems.md)  
- [Online Simulations and Tools](./01_Resources/Networking_Simulations.md)  

## Progress  
### Foundations  
- [ ] Fundamentals of Networking  
- [ ] Application Layer Protocols  

### Core Concepts  
- [ ] Transport Layer Mechanisms  
- [ ] Network Layer and Routing  
- [ ] Link Layer and LANs  

### Advanced Topics  
- [ ] Wireless and Mobile Networking  
- [ ] Security in Computer Networks  
- [ ] Multimedia Networking  

---

## Syllabus  

### 1. Fundamentals of Networking (Weeks 1-2)  
- **Topics**:  
  - Network Models (OSI, TCP/IP)  
  - Types of Networks (LAN, WAN, MAN)  
  - Network Topologies  
  - Internet Structure and ISPs  
  - Switching (Circuit, Packet, Message)  
  - Bandwidth, Latency, and Throughput  
- **Practice**: Compare network models, calculate throughput and latency  
- **Applications**: Internet architecture, network design  

---

### 2. Application Layer Protocols (Weeks 3-4)  
- **Topics**:  
  - HTTP, FTP, SMTP, and DNS  
  - Peer-to-Peer Applications  
  - Web and Email Protocols  
  - Content Distribution Networks  
  - Application Layer Security (TLS, HTTPS)  
- **Practice**: Simulate DNS resolution, analyze HTTP requests  
- **Applications**: Web development, client-server communication  

---

### 3. Transport Layer Mechanisms (Weeks 5-6)  
- **Topics**:  
  - Transport Layer Protocols (TCP, UDP)  
  - Reliable Data Transfer and Flow Control  
  - Congestion Control  
  - Connection-Oriented vs. Connectionless Services  
- **Practice**: Compare TCP and UDP, analyze packet flow  
- **Applications**: Real-time communication, application-layer protocols  

---

### 4. Network Layer and Routing (Weeks 7-8)  
- **Topics**:  
  - IP Addressing and Subnetting  
  - IPv4 vs. IPv6  
  - Routing Algorithms (Link-State, Distance Vector)  
  - NAT and DHCP  
  - Routing Protocols (RIP, OSPF, BGP)  
- **Practice**: Subnet networks, simulate routing tables  
- **Applications**: Network management, efficient data routing  

---

### 5. Link Layer and Local Area Networks (Weeks 9-10)  
- **Topics**:  
  - Ethernet Standards (IEEE 802.3)  
  - MAC Addressing and ARP  
  - Error Detection and Correction (CRC)  
  - Switching and VLANs  
- **Practice**: Configure VLANs, simulate ARP processes  
- **Applications**: LAN design, error mitigation  

---

### 6. Wireless and Mobile Networking (Weeks 11-12)  
- **Topics**:  
  - Wireless Standards (Wi-Fi, LTE, 5G)  
  - Wireless Security (WPA, WPA2)  
  - Mobile IP and Handoffs  
  - Ad Hoc and Sensor Networks  
- **Practice**: Analyze Wi-Fi protocols, simulate handoff processes  
- **Applications**: Mobile networks, IoT connectivity  

---

### 7. Security in Computer Networks (Weeks 13-14)  
- **Topics**:  
  - Cryptographic Techniques (Symmetric, Asymmetric)  
  - Firewalls and Intrusion Detection Systems  
  - Network Layer Security (IPSec)  
  - Secure Socket Layer (SSL) and VPNs  
- **Practice**: Configure a firewall, simulate SSL communication  
- **Applications**: Network security, secure communications  

---

### 8. Multimedia Networking (Weeks 15-16)  
- **Topics**:  
  - Multimedia Applications and QoS  
  - Streaming Protocols (RTP, RTSP)  
  - Voice over IP (VoIP)  
  - Multimedia Compression Standards (H.264, MPEG)  
- **Practice**: Analyze streaming protocols, configure VoIP  
- **Applications**: Video conferencing, media delivery  

---

### 9. Network Management (Weeks 17-18)  
- **Topics**:  
  - SNMP Protocol  
  - Network Monitoring and Troubleshooting  
  - Performance Metrics  
  - Managing Enterprise Networks  
- **Practice**: Simulate SNMP, analyze network performance data  
- **Applications**: Enterprise IT management, network administration  

---

### 10. Emerging Trends and Technologies (Weeks 19-20)  
- **Topics**:  
  - Software-Defined Networking (SDN)  
  - Network Virtualization  
  - Internet of Things (IoT) Protocols  
  - Cloud Networking and Edge Computing  
- **Practice**: Explore SDN tools, simulate IoT communication  
- **Applications**: Next-gen networking, smart systems-e 

---
**Up to:** [[Index]]

FILE: ./06_Machine_Learning_and_Artificial_Intelligence/001_Foundational_AI/001_Foundational_AI.md
════════════════════════════════════════
# Artificial Intelligence and Machine Learning 

### Key Topics and Notes
- [Foundations of AI MOC](./00_MOCs/01_Foundations_of_AI_MOC.md)
- [Problem-Solving Techniques MOC](./00_MOCs/02_Problem-Solving_Techniques_MOC.md)
- [Knowledge Representation and Reasoning MOC](./00_MOCs/03_Knowledge_Representation_and_Reasoning_MOC.md)
- [Planning and Decision Making MOC](./00_MOCs/04_Planning_and_Decision_Making_MOC.md)
- [Uncertainty in AI MOC](./00_MOCs/05_Uncertainty_in_AI_MOC.md)
- [Machine Learning Fundamentals MOC](./00_MOCs/06_Machine_Learning_Fundamentals_MOC.md)
- [Neural Networks and Deep Learning MOC](./00_MOCs/07_Neural_Networks_and_Deep_Learning_MOC.md)
- [Natural Language Processing NLP MOC](./00_MOCs/08_Natural_Language_Processing_NLP_MOC.md)
- [Robotics and Perception MOC](./00_MOCs/09_Robotics_and_Perception_MOC.md)
- [Multi-Agent Systems MOC](./00_MOCs/10_Multi-Agent_Systems_MOC.md)
- [Ethics and Future of AI MOC](./00_MOCs/11_Ethics_and_Future_of_AI_MOC.md)


## Resources  
- [Ebook: Artificial Intelligence: A Modern Approach, Stuart Russell and Peter Norvig](./01_Resources/01_Artificial_Intelligence_ A_Modern_Approach_Russell_4th.pdf)  
- [Practice Problems and Projects](./02_Practice-Problems.md)  

## Progress  
### Foundations  
- [ ] Foundations of AI  
- [ ] Problem-Solving Techniques  

### Core Concepts  
- [ ] Knowledge Representation and Reasoning  
- [ ] Planning and Decision Making  
- [ ] Machine Learning Fundamentals  

### Advanced Topics  
- [ ] Neural Networks and Deep Learning  
- [ ] Natural Language Processing  
- [ ] Robotics and Perception  

---

## Syllabus  

### 1. Foundations of AI (Weeks 1-2)  
- **Topics**:  
  - Definition of AI  
  - History and Scope of AI  
  - Intelligent Agents  
  - AI Research Trends  
- **Practice**: Analyze AI case studies and applications  
- **Applications**: Autonomous systems, expert systems  

---

### 2. Problem-Solving Techniques (Weeks 3-4)  
- **Topics**:  
  - Search Strategies (Uninformed, Informed)  
  - Adversarial Search and Game Playing  
  - Constraint Satisfaction Problems  
  - Optimization Techniques  
- **Practice**: Solve problems using search algorithms  
- **Applications**: Pathfinding, decision-making  

---

### 3. Knowledge Representation and Reasoning (Weeks 5-6)  
- **Topics**:  
  - Propositional and Predicate Logic  
  - Ontologies and Semantic Networks  
  - Knowledge-Based Systems  
  - Reasoning Under Uncertainty  
- **Practice**: Design knowledge bases and implement reasoning systems  
- **Applications**: Natural language understanding, expert systems  

---

### 4. Planning and Decision Making (Weeks 7-8)  
- **Topics**:  
  - Classical Planning Techniques  
  - Heuristics for Planning  
  - Decision Theory  
  - Markov Decision Processes (MDPs)  
- **Practice**: Create plans for complex tasks  
- **Applications**: Robotics, scheduling  

---

### 5. Uncertainty in AI (Weeks 9-10)  
- **Topics**:  
  - Bayesian Networks  
  - Probabilistic Reasoning  
  - Hidden Markov Models (HMMs)  
  - Fuzzy Logic  
- **Practice**: Solve problems using probabilistic models  
- **Applications**: Speech recognition, recommendation systems  

---

### 6. Machine Learning Fundamentals (Weeks 11-12)  
- **Topics**:  
  - Supervised Learning (Regression, Classification)  
  - Unsupervised Learning (Clustering, Dimensionality Reduction)  
  - Model Evaluation and Validation  
  - Feature Engineering  
- **Practice**: Train machine learning models  
- **Applications**: Data analytics, predictive modeling  

---

### 7. Neural Networks and Deep Learning (Weeks 13-14)  
- **Topics**:  
  - Basics of Neural Networks  
  - Convolutional Neural Networks (CNNs)  
  - Recurrent Neural Networks (RNNs)  
  - Optimization Techniques in Deep Learning  
- **Practice**: Implement neural networks for image and sequence data  
- **Applications**: Computer vision, time-series analysis  

---

### 8. Natural Language Processing (Weeks 15-16)  
- **Topics**:  
  - Tokenization and Text Preprocessing  
  - Word Embeddings (Word2Vec, GloVe)  
  - Sequence-to-Sequence Models  
  - Applications (Sentiment Analysis, Machine Translation)  
- **Practice**: Build NLP pipelines and train models  
- **Applications**: Language understanding, chatbots  

---

### 9. Robotics and Perception (Weeks 17-18)  
- **Topics**:  
  - Perception Systems (Sensors, Computer Vision)  
  - Motion and Path Planning  
  - Robot Control Architectures  
  - Human-Robot Interaction  
- **Practice**: Simulate robotic tasks and control systems  
- **Applications**: Autonomous vehicles, industrial robots  

---

### 10. Multi-Agent Systems (Weeks 19-20)  
- **Topics**:  
  - Coordination and Cooperation  
  - Game Theory in AI  
  - Distributed AI  
  - Applications of Multi-Agent Systems  
- **Practice**: Design and analyze multi-agent simulations  
- **Applications**: Networked systems, collaborative robotics  

---

### 11. Ethics and Future of AI (Weeks 21-22)  
- **Topics**:  
  - Ethical Considerations in AI Development  
  - AI and Job Automation  
  - Safety and Accountability  
  - AI for Social Good  
- **Practice**: Write essays on AI ethics and present case studies  
- **Applications**: Policy development, ethical AI design-e 

---
**Up to:** [[Index]]

FILE: ./06_Machine_Learning_and_Artificial_Intelligence/01_Supervised_Learning/01_Supervised_Learning.md
════════════════════════════════════════
# Supervised Learning

### Key Topics and Notes
- [Basics of Machine Learning MOC](./00_MOCs/01_Basics_of_Machine_Learning_MOC.md)
- [Linear Models for Regression and Classification MOC](./00_MOCs/02_Linear_Models_for_Regression_and_Classification_MOC.md)
- [Probabilistic Models and Bayesian Inference MOC](./00_MOCs/03_Probabilistic_Models_and_Bayesian_Inference_MOC.md)
- [Neural Networks MOC](./00_MOCs/04_Neural_Networks_MOC.md)
- [Kernel Methods and Support Vector Machines MOC](./00_MOCs/05_Kernel_Methods_and_Support_Vector_Machines_MOC.md)
- [Ensemble Learning MOC](./00_MOCs/06_Ensemble_Learning_MOC.md)
- [Feature Selection and Dimensionality Reduction MOC](./00_MOCs/07_Feature_Selection_and_Dimensionality_Reduction_MOC.md)
- [Model Evaluation and Validation MOC](./00_MOCs/08_Model_Evaluation_and_Validation_MOC.md)
- [Generative Models MOC](./00_MOCs/09_Generative_Models_MOC.md)
- [Applications and Case Studies MOC](./00_MOCs/10_Applications_and_Case_Studies_MOC.md)


## Resources  
- [Ebook: Pattern Recognition and Machine Learning, Christopher M. Bishop](./01_Resources/01_Pattern_Recognition_and_Machine_Learning_Bishop.pdf)    
- [Practice Problems and Datasets](./02_Practice-Problems.md)  

## Progress  
### Foundations  
- [ ] Basics of Machine Learning  
- [ ] Linear Models  

### Core Concepts  
- [ ] Probabilistic Models  
- [ ] Neural Networks  
- [ ] Kernel Methods  

### Advanced Topics  
- [ ] Ensemble Learning  
- [ ] Dimensionality Reduction  
- [ ] Generative Models  

---

## Syllabus  

### 1. Basics of Machine Learning (Weeks 1-2)  
- **Topics**:  
  - Types of Learning: Supervised, Unsupervised, Reinforcement  
  - Key Concepts: Training, Validation, Testing  
  - Common Algorithms Overview  
  - Bias-Variance Tradeoff  
  - Performance Metrics (Accuracy, Precision, Recall, F1 Score)  
- **Practice**: Analyze simple datasets using linear regression and classification  
- **Applications**: Predictive modeling, data-driven decision-making  

---

### 2. Linear Models for Regression and Classification (Weeks 3-4)  
- **Topics**:  
  - Linear Regression  
  - Logistic Regression  
  - Regularization: Ridge and Lasso  
  - Gradient Descent  
  - Multi-class Classification  
- **Practice**: Implement linear and logistic regression on real-world datasets  
- **Applications**: Predictive analytics, risk modeling  

---

### 3. Probabilistic Models and Bayesian Inference (Weeks 5-6)  
- **Topics**:  
  - Probability Distributions  
  - Maximum Likelihood Estimation (MLE)  
  - Bayesian Inference  
  - Naive Bayes Classifier  
  - Hidden Markov Models (HMMs)  
- **Practice**: Solve problems involving MLE and Bayesian inference  
- **Applications**: Spam filtering, time-series analysis  

---

### 4. Neural Networks (Weeks 7-8)  
- **Topics**:  
  - Perceptron and Multilayer Perceptrons (MLPs)  
  - Activation Functions  
  - Backpropagation and Gradient Descent  
  - Overfitting and Regularization in Neural Networks  
  - Introduction to Deep Learning  
- **Practice**: Build simple neural networks using libraries like TensorFlow or PyTorch  
- **Applications**: Image recognition, speech processing  

---

### 5. Kernel Methods and Support Vector Machines (Weeks 9-10)  
- **Topics**:  
  - Kernel Functions  
  - Support Vector Machines (SVMs)  
  - Soft Margin Classification  
  - Applications of SVM in High-Dimensional Spaces  
- **Practice**: Implement SVMs with kernels for non-linear classification  
- **Applications**: Bioinformatics, text categorization  

---

### 6. Ensemble Learning (Weeks 11-12)  
- **Topics**:  
  - Bagging and Boosting  
  - Random Forests  
  - Gradient Boosted Machines (XGBoost)  
  - Stacking Models  
- **Practice**: Develop ensemble models for complex datasets  
- **Applications**: Fraud detection, recommendation systems  

---

### 7. Feature Selection and Dimensionality Reduction (Weeks 13-14)  
- **Topics**:  
  - Principal Component Analysis (PCA)  
  - Singular Value Decomposition (SVD)  
  - Feature Engineering  
  - L1 Regularization for Feature Selection  
  - t-SNE and UMAP for Visualization  
- **Practice**: Apply PCA and feature selection on large datasets  
- **Applications**: Preprocessing for high-dimensional data  

---

### 8. Model Evaluation and Validation (Weeks 15-16)  
- **Topics**:  
  - Cross-Validation Techniques  
  - Hyperparameter Tuning  
  - Grid Search and Random Search  
  - Model Interpretability  
  - Handling Imbalanced Datasets  
- **Practice**: Perform cross-validation and tune models for better performance  
- **Applications**: Deployment of robust models in production  

---

### 9. Generative Models (Weeks 17-18)  
- **Topics**:  
  - Gaussian Mixture Models  
  - Generative Adversarial Networks (GANs)  
  - Variational Autoencoders (VAEs)  
  - Applications in Image and Text Generation  
- **Practice**: Train simple GANs and VAEs for data generation tasks  
- **Applications**: Synthetic data generation, anomaly detection  

---

### 10. Applications and Case Studies (Weeks 19-20)  
- **Topics**:  
  - Practical Machine Learning Pipelines  
  - Case Studies in Healthcare, Finance, and Technology  
  - Deployment of Machine Learning Models  
  - Ethical Considerations in Machine Learning  
- **Practice**: Solve end-to-end projects and analyze case studies  
- **Applications**: Industry-specific problem solving, real-world applications-e 

---
**Up to:** [[Index]]

FILE: ./06_Machine_Learning_and_Artificial_Intelligence/02_Unsupervised_Learning/02_Unsupervised_Learning.md
════════════════════════════════════════
# Unsupervised Learning 

### Key Topics and Notes
- [Introduction to Unsupervised Learning MOC](./00_MOCs/01_Introduction_to_Unsupervised_Learning_MOC.md)
- [Clustering Methods MOC](./00_MOCs/02_Clustering_Methods_MOC.md)
- [Dimensionality Reduction MOC](./00_MOCs/03_Dimensionality_Reduction_MOC.md)
- [Gaussian Mixture Models MOC](./00_MOCs/04_Gaussian_Mixture_Models_MOC.md)
- [Hidden Markov Models MOC](./00_MOCs/05_Hidden_Markov_Models_MOC.md)
- [Principal Component Analysis PCA MOC](./00_MOCs/06_Principal_Component_Analysis_PCA_MOC.md)
- [Self-Organizing Maps MOC](./00_MOCs/07_Self-Organizing_Maps_MOC.md)
- [k-Means Clustering MOC](./00_MOCs/08_k-Means_Clustering_MOC.md)
- [Density-Based Clustering MOC](./00_MOCs/09_Density-Based_Clustering_MOC.md)
- [Expectation Maximization MOC](./00_MOCs/10_Expectation_Maximization_MOC.md)
- [Feature Selection and Engineering MOC](./00_MOCs/11_Feature_Selection_and_Engineering_MOC.md)
- [Anomaly Detection MOC](./00_MOCs/12_Anomaly_Detection_MOC.md)
- [Evaluation of Unsupervised Models MOC](./00_MOCs/13_Evaluation_of_Unsupervised_Models_MOC.md)
- [Graph-Based Methods MOC](./00_MOCs/14_Graph-Based_Methods_MOC.md)


## Resources  
- [Ebook: Introduction to Statistical Learning](./01_Resources/01_An_Introduction_to_Statistical_Learning_Gareth.pdf)
- [Lecture Notes: Unsupervised Learning, Stanford](./01_Resources/02_Unsupervised_Learning_Stanford.pdf)  
- [Practice Problems: Unsupervised Learning](./02_Practice-Problems.md)  
- [Software Tools and Tutorials](./01_Resources/Software_Tools.md)  

## Progress  
### Foundations  
- [ ] Introduction to Unsupervised Learning  
- [ ] Clustering Methods  
- [ ] Dimensionality Reduction  

### Core Concepts  
- [ ] Gaussian Mixture Models  
- [ ] Hidden Markov Models  
- [ ] Principal Component Analysis (PCA)  
- [ ] k-Means Clustering  

### Advanced Topics  
- [ ] Expectation Maximization  
- [ ] Feature Selection and Engineering  
- [ ] Anomaly Detection  

---

## Syllabus  

### 1. Introduction to Unsupervised Learning (Weeks 1-2)  
- **Topics**:  
  - Overview of Machine Learning  
  - Types of Learning: Supervised, Unsupervised, Reinforcement  
  - The Role of Unsupervised Learning in Data Science  
  - Key Applications of Unsupervised Learning  
- **Practice**: Implement basic unsupervised learning algorithms  
- **Applications**: Data mining, pattern recognition, customer segmentation  

---

### 2. Clustering Methods (Weeks 3-4)  
- **Topics**:  
  - Introduction to Clustering  
  - Hierarchical Clustering  
  - k-Means Clustering  
  - DBSCAN (Density-Based Spatial Clustering)  
  - Evaluation Metrics for Clustering (Silhouette Score, Inertia)  
- **Practice**: Implement k-means and DBSCAN on sample datasets  
- **Applications**: Market segmentation, image compression, bioinformatics  

---

### 3. Dimensionality Reduction (Weeks 5-6)  
- **Topics**:  
  - The Curse of Dimensionality  
  - Principal Component Analysis (PCA)  
  - t-SNE for Visualization  
  - Linear Discriminant Analysis (LDA)  
- **Practice**: Apply PCA and t-SNE to reduce dimensionality in datasets  
- **Applications**: Feature selection, noise reduction, visualization of high-dimensional data  

---

### 4. Gaussian Mixture Models (Weeks 7-8)  
- **Topics**:  
  - Gaussian Distribution and its Properties  
  - Gaussian Mixture Models (GMM)  
  - Expectation Maximization Algorithm  
  - Model Selection and Evaluation for GMM  
- **Practice**: Implement GMM on real-world data  
- **Applications**: Image segmentation, speech recognition, anomaly detection  

---

### 5. Hidden Markov Models (Weeks 9-10)  
- **Topics**:  
  - Introduction to Markov Chains  
  - Hidden Markov Models (HMM)  
  - Forward-Backward Algorithm  
  - Viterbi Algorithm for State Estimation  
- **Practice**: Build HMM for time-series data prediction  
- **Applications**: Speech recognition, bioinformatics, financial modeling  

---

### 6. Self-Organizing Maps (Weeks 11-12)  
- **Topics**:  
  - Introduction to Neural Networks  
  - Self-Organizing Maps (SOM) Overview  
  - SOM Learning Algorithm  
  - Visualization of High-Dimensional Data using SOM  
- **Practice**: Implement SOM to classify and visualize data  
- **Applications**: Data visualization, clustering, feature extraction  

---

### 7. k-Means Clustering (Weeks 13-14)  
- **Topics**:  
  - k-Means Algorithm and Initialization Methods  
  - Convergence Criteria in k-Means  
  - k-Means vs k-Medoids  
  - Extensions of k-Means (Mini-batch k-Means, Fuzzy k-Means)  
- **Practice**: Tuning k-Means for optimal clustering results  
- **Applications**: Customer segmentation, anomaly detection, image segmentation  

---

### 8. Density-Based Clustering (Weeks 15-16)  
- **Topics**:  
  - Density-Based Clustering and DBSCAN  
  - DBSCAN vs k-Means  
  - HDBSCAN (Hierarchical DBSCAN)  
  - OPTICS Algorithm  
- **Practice**: Apply DBSCAN and HDBSCAN to complex datasets  
- **Applications**: Spatial data analysis, image clustering, geospatial data  

---

### 9. Expectation Maximization (Weeks 17-18)  
- **Topics**:  
  - Overview of EM Algorithm  
  - Maximization Step and Expectation Step  
  - Applications of EM in GMM and Hidden Markov Models  
  - Model Selection and Evaluation  
- **Practice**: Implement Expectation Maximization in various clustering tasks  
- **Applications**: Speech and image recognition, anomaly detection  

---

### 10. Feature Selection and Engineering (Weeks 19-20)  
- **Topics**:  
  - Importance of Feature Selection  
  - Filter, Wrapper, and Embedded Methods  
  - Feature Engineering Techniques (One-hot Encoding, Normalization)  
  - Dimensionality Reduction vs Feature Selection  
- **Practice**: Apply feature selection techniques to improve clustering models  
- **Applications**: Data preprocessing, model optimization, pattern discovery  

---

### 11. Anomaly Detection (Weeks 21-22)  
- **Topics**:  
  - What is Anomaly Detection?  
  - Types of Anomalies (Point, Contextual, Collective)  
  - Anomaly Detection Algorithms (Isolation Forest, One-Class SVM)  
  - Evaluation Metrics for Anomaly Detection  
- **Practice**: Implement anomaly detection algorithms on real-world datasets  
- **Applications**: Fraud detection, network security, industrial monitoring  

---

### 12. Evaluation of Unsupervised Models (Weeks 23-24)  
- **Topics**:  
  - Metrics for Evaluating Clustering Models (Silhouette Score, Adjusted Rand Index)  
  - Cross-validation in Unsupervised Learning  
  - Model Tuning and Selection  
- **Practice**: Use different evaluation methods to assess model quality  
- **Applications**: Model selection, performance analysis  

---

### 13. Graph-Based Methods (Weeks 25-26)  
- **Topics**:  
  - Introduction to Graph Theory and Graph-Based Clustering  
  - Spectral Clustering  
  - Community Detection Algorithms  
  - Graph-Based Dimensionality Reduction (Laplacian Eigenmaps)  
- **Practice**: Implement graph-based clustering algorithms  
- **Applications**: Social network analysis, recommendation systems, bioinformatics-e 

---
**Up to:** [[Index]]

FILE: ./06_Machine_Learning_and_Artificial_Intelligence/03_Deep_Learning/03_Deep_Learning.md
════════════════════════════════════════
# Deep Learning 

### Key Topics and Notes
- [Neural Networks and Deep Learning Foundations MOC](./00_MOCs/01_Neural_Networks_and_Deep_Learning_Foundations_MOC.md)
- [Activation Functions MOC](./00_MOCs/02_Activation_Functions_MOC.md)
- [Gradient Descent and Backpropagation MOC](./00_MOCs/03_Gradient_Descent_and_Backpropagation_MOC.md)
- [Convolutional Neural Networks CNNs MOC](./00_MOCs/04_Convolutional_Neural_Networks_CNNs_MOC.md)
- [Recurrent Neural Networks RNNs and LSTMs MOC](./00_MOCs/05_Recurrent_Neural_Networks_RNNs_and_LSTMs_MOC.md)
- [Generative Models GANs VAEs MOC](./00_MOCs/06_Generative_Models_GANs_VAEs_MOC.md)
- [Reinforcement Learning MOC](./00_MOCs/07_Reinforcement_Learning_MOC.md)
- [Optimization Algorithms MOC](./00_MOCs/08_Optimization_Algorithms_MOC.md)
- [Regularization Techniques MOC](./00_MOCs/09_Regularization_Techniques_MOC.md)
- [Hyperparameter Tuning MOC](./00_MOCs/10_Hyperparameter_Tuning_MOC.md)
- [Transfer Learning MOC](./00_MOCs/11_Transfer_Learning_MOC.md)
- [Advanced Neural Network Architectures MOC](./00_MOCs/12_Advanced_Neural_Network_Architectures_MOC.md)
- [Natural Language Processing NLP with Deep Learning MOC](./00_MOCs/13_Natural_Language_Processing_NLP_with_Deep_Learning_MOC.md)
- [Computer Vision with Deep Learning MOC](./00_MOCs/14_Computer_Vision_with_Deep_Learning_MOC.md)
- [Model Deployment and Scalability MOC](./00_MOCs/15_Model_Deployment_and_Scalability_MOC.md)

## Resources  
- [Ebook: Deep Learning, Ian Goodfellow, Yoshua Bengio, Aaron Courville](./01_Resources/01_Deep_Learning_Ian_.pdf)    
- [Practice Problems and Code](./02_Practice-Problems.md)  
- [Deep Learning Frameworks: TensorFlow, PyTorch](./01_Resources/Deep_Learning_Frameworks.md)  

## Progress  
### Foundations  
- [ ] Neural Networks and Deep Learning Foundations  
- [ ] Activation Functions  
- [ ] Gradient Descent and Backpropagation  

### Core Concepts  
- [ ] CNNs and Image Processing  
- [ ] RNNs and Sequence Models  
- [ ] Generative Models  

### Advanced Topics  
- [ ] Optimization Algorithms  
- [ ] Regularization and Hyperparameter Tuning  
- [ ] NLP and Computer Vision  
- [ ] Model Deployment  

---

## Syllabus  

### 1. Neural Networks and Deep Learning Foundations (Weeks 1-2)  
- **Topics**:  
  - Overview of Neural Networks  
  - Perceptrons and Multi-layer Perceptrons (MLPs)  
  - Feedforward Neural Networks  
  - Training Neural Networks: Objective Functions  
  - Introduction to Deep Learning  
- **Practice**: Implement simple neural networks using Python  
- **Applications**: Image classification, basic machine learning models  

---

### 2. Activation Functions (Weeks 3-4)  
- **Topics**:  
  - Sigmoid, Tanh, ReLU  
  - Leaky ReLU, ELU  
  - Softmax  
  - Activation Functions and Their Impact on Learning  
- **Practice**: Experiment with different activation functions in neural networks  
- **Applications**: Optimizing deep learning models for faster convergence  

---

### 3. Gradient Descent and Backpropagation (Weeks 5-6)  
- **Topics**:  
  - Understanding Gradient Descent  
  - Stochastic Gradient Descent (SGD)  
  - Backpropagation Algorithm  
  - Learning Rate and Optimization Techniques  
- **Practice**: Implement gradient descent and backpropagation from scratch  
- **Applications**: Training neural networks, optimization in machine learning  

---

### 4. Convolutional Neural Networks (CNNs) (Weeks 7-8)  
- **Topics**:  
  - Convolutional Layers  
  - Pooling Layers (Max Pooling, Average Pooling)  
  - CNN Architectures (LeNet, AlexNet, VGG)  
  - Transfer Learning with CNNs  
- **Practice**: Build and train CNNs for image classification tasks  
- **Applications**: Computer vision, object detection, and image recognition  

---

### 5. Recurrent Neural Networks (RNNs) and LSTMs (Weeks 9-10)  
- **Topics**:  
  - RNNs: Architecture and Training  
  - Challenges with RNNs (Vanishing/Exploding Gradients)  
  - Long Short-Term Memory (LSTM) Networks  
  - Gated Recurrent Units (GRUs)  
- **Practice**: Implement RNNs and LSTMs for time series and sequence data  
- **Applications**: Natural Language Processing, speech recognition  

---

### 6. Generative Models (Weeks 11-12)  
- **Topics**:  
  - Variational Autoencoders (VAEs)  
  - Generative Adversarial Networks (GANs)  
  - Training GANs  
  - Applications of GANs (Image Generation, Data Augmentation)  
- **Practice**: Implement VAEs and GANs for generating synthetic data  
- **Applications**: Data synthesis, art generation, anomaly detection  

---

### 7. Optimization Algorithms (Weeks 13-14)  
- **Topics**:  
  - Gradient Descent Variants (Momentum, AdaGrad, Adam)  
  - Learning Rate Schedules  
  - Batch Normalization  
  - Adaptive Optimizers  
- **Practice**: Experiment with various optimizers to improve model performance  
- **Applications**: Efficient training of deep learning models  

---

### 8. Regularization Techniques (Weeks 15-16)  
- **Topics**:  
  - Overfitting and Underfitting  
  - Dropout Regularization  
  - L2 and L1 Regularization  
  - Early Stopping  
- **Practice**: Apply regularization to improve model generalization  
- **Applications**: Model robustness, preventing overfitting  

---

### 9. Hyperparameter Tuning (Weeks 17-18)  
- **Topics**:  
  - Grid Search and Random Search  
  - Hyperparameter Optimization (Bayesian, Hyperband)  
  - Cross-Validation Techniques  
  - Performance Metrics for Deep Learning Models  
- **Practice**: Optimize deep learning models using hyperparameter tuning methods  
- **Applications**: Model performance improvement, computational efficiency  

---

### 10. Transfer Learning (Weeks 19-20)  
- **Topics**:  
  - Concept of Transfer Learning  
  - Fine-Tuning Pre-trained Models  
  - Applications of Transfer Learning in CNNs  
  - Transfer Learning for NLP Models (BERT, GPT)  
- **Practice**: Fine-tune pre-trained models for specific tasks  
- **Applications**: Real-world applications with limited data  

---

### 11. Natural Language Processing (NLP) with Deep Learning (Weeks 21-22)  
- **Topics**:  
  - Word Embeddings (Word2Vec, GloVe)  
  - Sequence-to-Sequence Models  
  - Transformers and Attention Mechanisms  
  - BERT, GPT, and Large Language Models  
- **Practice**: Implement NLP models for sentiment analysis and translation  
- **Applications**: Text analysis, chatbots, language translation  

---

### 12. Computer Vision with Deep Learning (Weeks 23-24)  
- **Topics**:  
  - Image Preprocessing and Augmentation  
  - Object Detection Algorithms (YOLO, Faster R-CNN)  
  - Semantic Segmentation  
  - Image Captioning  
- **Practice**: Build computer vision models for object detection and segmentation  
- **Applications**: Autonomous driving, medical imaging  

---

### 13. Model Deployment and Scalability (Weeks 25-26)  
- **Topics**:  
  - Model Deployment with TensorFlow Serving, TorchServe  
  - Optimizing Models for Production (Quantization, Pruning)  
  - Cloud Deployment (AWS, Google Cloud AI)  
  - Building Scalable Deep Learning Systems  
- **Practice**: Deploy models to cloud platforms and optimize for production  
- **Applications**: Real-time AI applications, large-scale machine learning solutions-e 

---
**Up to:** [[Index]]

FILE: ./06_Machine_Learning_and_Artificial_Intelligence/04_Reinforcement_Learning/04_Reinforcement_Learning.md
════════════════════════════════════════
# Reinforcement Learning

### Key Topics and Notes
- [Fundamentals of Reinforcement Learning MOC](./00_MOCs/01_Fundamentals_of_Reinforcement_Learning_MOC.md)
- [Evaluative Feedback and Action-Value Methods MOC](./00_MOCs/02_Evaluative_Feedback_and_Action-Value_Methods_MOC.md)
- [Markov Decision Processes MOC](./00_MOCs/03_Markov_Decision_Processes_MOC.md)
- [Dynamic Programming and Monte Carlo Methods MOC](./00_MOCs/04_Dynamic_Programming_and_Monte_Carlo_Methods_MOC.md)
- [Temporal-Difference Learning and Actor-Critic Methods MOC](./00_MOCs/05_Temporal-Difference_Learning_and_Actor-Critic_Methods_MOC.md)
- [Eligibility Traces and Function Approximation MOC](./00_MOCs/06_Eligibility_Traces_and_Function_Approximation_MOC.md)
- [Planning and Learning in Reinforcement Learning MOC](./00_MOCs/07_Planning_and_Learning_in_Reinforcement_Learning_MOC.md)
- [Case Studies and Real-World Applications MOC](./00_MOCs/08_Case_Studies_and_Real-World_Applications_MOC.md)


## Resources  
- [Ebook: Reinforcement Learning: An Introduction, Richard S. Sutton](./01_Resources/01_Reinforcement_Learning_Sutton_.pdf)  
- [Practice Problems](./02_Practice-Problems.md)  
- [Reinforcement Learning Code Examples](./01_Resources/RL_Code_Examples.md)  

## Progress  
### Foundations  
- [ ] Introduction to Reinforcement Learning  
- [ ] Markov Decision Processes  
- [ ] Evaluative Feedback and Action-Value Methods  

### Core Concepts  
- [ ] Dynamic Programming  
- [ ] Monte Carlo Methods  
- [ ] Temporal-Difference Learning  

### Advanced Topics  
- [ ] Eligibility Traces  
- [ ] Function Approximation  
- [ ] Planning and Learning  
- [ ] Case Studies and Applications  

---

## Syllabus  

### 1. Introduction to Reinforcement Learning (Weeks 1-2)  
- **Topics**:  
  - Overview of Reinforcement Learning  
  - Key Elements: Agent, Environment, Rewards  
  - Examples: Tic-Tac-Toe, Slot Machines  
  - The Problem of Reinforcement Learning  
- **Practice**: Implement basic RL agents for simple games like Tic-Tac-Toe  
- **Applications**: Introduction to the core of RL, games and simulations  

---

### 2. Evaluative Feedback and Action-Value Methods (Weeks 3-4)  
- **Topics**:  
  - The n-Armed Bandit Problem  
  - Action-Value Methods and Softmax Action Selection  
  - Optimistic Initial Values and Pursuit Methods  
  - Tracking Nonstationary Problems  
- **Practice**: Implement the n-Armed Bandit problem using action-value methods  
- **Applications**: Exploration and exploitation in decision-making  

---

### 3. Markov Decision Processes (Weeks 5-6)  
- **Topics**:  
  - Agent-Environment Interface  
  - Goals, Rewards, and Returns  
  - The Markov Property and Markov Decision Processes  
  - Value Functions and Optimal Value Functions  
- **Practice**: Model a reinforcement learning problem as an MDP and calculate value functions  
- **Applications**: Structuring RL problems for optimal decision-making  

---

### 4. Dynamic Programming (Weeks 7-8)  
- **Topics**:  
  - Policy Evaluation and Improvement  
  - Policy Iteration and Value Iteration  
  - Generalized Policy Iteration and Efficiency  
- **Practice**: Implement Dynamic Programming algorithms like Policy Iteration  
- **Applications**: Solving reinforcement learning problems with complete knowledge  

---

### 5. Monte Carlo Methods (Weeks 9-10)  
- **Topics**:  
  - Monte Carlo Policy Evaluation  
  - Action-Value Estimation  
  - On-Policy and Off-Policy Monte Carlo Control  
  - Incremental Implementation of Monte Carlo Methods  
- **Practice**: Implement Monte Carlo methods for evaluating policies  
- **Applications**: Learning through experience-based sampling  

---

### 6. Temporal-Difference Learning (Weeks 11-12)  
- **Topics**:  
  - TD Prediction and Advantages of TD Methods  
  - Sarsa and Q-Learning for On-Policy and Off-Policy Control  
  - Actor-Critic Methods and R-Learning  
- **Practice**: Implement TD(0) prediction, Sarsa, and Q-Learning algorithms  
- **Applications**: Real-time learning and control for RL agents  

---

### 7. Eligibility Traces and n-Step TD (Weeks 13-14)  
- **Topics**:  
  - Eligibility Traces: Forward and Backward Views  
  - Sarsa(λ) and Q(λ)  
  - Replacing Traces for More Efficient Learning  
- **Practice**: Implement n-step TD and explore the forward and backward views  
- **Applications**: Efficient learning in long-term tasks  

---

### 8. Function Approximation in Reinforcement Learning (Weeks 15-16)  
- **Topics**:  
  - Value Prediction with Function Approximation  
  - Gradient Descent Methods and Linear Methods  
  - Control with Function Approximation  
  - Off-Policy Bootstrapping  
- **Practice**: Implement RL algorithms with function approximation for complex problems  
- **Applications**: Handling large state spaces through function approximation  

---

### 9. Planning and Learning (Weeks 17-18)  
- **Topics**:  
  - Models and Planning in RL  
  - Integrating Planning, Acting, and Learning  
  - Prioritized Sweeping and Sample Backups  
  - Heuristic Search for Improved Planning  
- **Practice**: Implement prioritized sweeping and trajectory sampling  
- **Applications**: Integrating learning with strategic planning  

---

### 10. Case Studies in Reinforcement Learning (Weeks 19-20)  
- **Topics**:  
  - TD-Gammon (Backgammon Example)  
  - Samuel’s Checkers Player  
  - The Acrobot and Elevator Dispatching Problems  
  - Dynamic Channel Allocation and Job-Shop Scheduling  
- **Practice**: Analyze RL case studies and implement solutions  
- **Applications**: Real-world applications in game-playing, optimization, and scheduling-e 

---
**Up to:** [[Index]]

FILE: ./06_Machine_Learning_and_Artificial_Intelligence/05_Python_for_ML/05_Python_for_ML.md
════════════════════════════════════════
# Python for Machine Learning

### Key Topics and Notes
- [Python Fundamentals for Machine Learning MOC](./00_MOCs/01_Python_Fundamentals_for_Machine_Learning_MOC.md)
- [Data Preprocessing and Feature Engineering MOC](./00_MOCs/02_Data_Preprocessing_and_Feature_Engineering_MOC.md)
- [Supervised Learning MOC](./00_MOCs/03_Supervised_Learning_MOC.md)
- [Unsupervised Learning MOC](./00_MOCs/04_Unsupervised_Learning_MOC.md)
- [Neural Networks and Deep Learning MOC](./00_MOCs/05_Neural_Networks_and_Deep_Learning_MOC.md)
- [Model Evaluation and Hyperparameter Tuning MOC](./00_MOCs/06_Model_Evaluation_and_Hyperparameter_Tuning_MOC.md)
- [Natural Language Processing MOC](./00_MOCs/07_Natural_Language_Processing_MOC.md)
- [Reinforcement Learning MOC](./00_MOCs/08_Reinforcement_Learning_MOC.md)
- [Dimensionality Reduction MOC](./00_MOCs/09_Dimensionality_Reduction_MOC.md)
- [Model Deployment MOC](./00_MOCs/10_Model_Deployment_MOC.md)

## Resources  
- [Ebook: Hands-On Machine Learning with Scikit-Learn, Keras, and TensorFlow, Aurélien Géron](./01_Resources/01_Hands_on_ML_Geron.pdf)  
- [Ebook: Python Machine Learning, Sebastian Raschka](./01_Resources/02_Python_Machine_Learning_Raschka.pdf)  
- [Practice Problems](./02_Practice-Problems.md)  
- [Python Code Examples](./01_Resources/Code_Examples.md)  

## Progress  
### Foundations  
- [ ] Python Basics  
- [ ] Data Preprocessing  

### Core Concepts  
- [ ] Supervised Learning  
- [ ] Unsupervised Learning  
- [ ] Neural Networks  

### Advanced Topics  
- [ ] Model Evaluation and Tuning  
- [ ] NLP and Reinforcement Learning  
- [ ] Dimensionality Reduction  
---

## Syllabus  

### 1. Python Basics for Machine Learning (Weeks 1-2)  
- **Topics**:  
  - Python Programming Fundamentals (Data types, functions, loops, conditionals)  
  - Numpy and Pandas for Data Manipulation  
  - Matplotlib and Seaborn for Data Visualization  
  - Introduction to Jupyter Notebooks  
  - Introduction to Scikit-Learn  
- **Practice**: Write Python scripts to load and manipulate data, visualize datasets  
- **Applications**: Data wrangling, visualizing datasets for analysis  

---

### 2. Data Preprocessing and Feature Engineering (Weeks 3-4)  
- **Topics**:  
  - Data Cleaning (Handling Missing Data, Outliers, Categorical Data)  
  - Feature Scaling (Normalization, Standardization)  
  - Feature Selection and Extraction  
  - Handling Imbalanced Datasets  
- **Practice**: Clean and preprocess real-world datasets for machine learning  
- **Applications**: Preprocessing data for training machine learning models  

---

### 3. Supervised Learning (Weeks 5-6)  
- **Topics**:  
  - Regression (Linear, Polynomial, Ridge, Lasso)  
  - Classification Algorithms (Logistic Regression, K-Nearest Neighbors, Support Vector Machines)  
  - Decision Trees and Random Forests  
  - Gradient Boosting (XGBoost, LightGBM)  
  - Model Evaluation Metrics (Accuracy, Precision, Recall, F1-Score)  
- **Practice**: Implement regression and classification models, evaluate their performance  
- **Applications**: Predicting numerical outcomes, classifying data into categories  

---

### 4. Unsupervised Learning (Weeks 7-8)  
- **Topics**:  
  - Clustering Algorithms (K-Means, Hierarchical Clustering, DBSCAN)  
  - Dimensionality Reduction Techniques (PCA, t-SNE)  
  - Anomaly Detection  
  - Association Rule Learning (Apriori Algorithm)  
- **Practice**: Implement clustering algorithms and evaluate clustering results  
- **Applications**: Grouping data without labels, dimensionality reduction for visualization  

---

### 5. Neural Networks and Deep Learning (Weeks 9-10)  
- **Topics**:  
  - Introduction to Neural Networks (Perceptrons, Activation Functions)  
  - Backpropagation and Gradient Descent  
  - Deep Learning Architectures (Feedforward, CNN, RNN)  
  - Introduction to Keras and TensorFlow  
  - Training Deep Learning Models  
- **Practice**: Build and train simple neural networks for classification tasks  
- **Applications**: Image recognition, time series forecasting  

---

### 6. Model Evaluation and Hyperparameter Tuning (Weeks 11-12)  
- **Topics**:  
  - Cross-Validation and Bias-Variance Tradeoff  
  - Hyperparameter Tuning (Grid Search, Random Search)  
  - Overfitting and Underfitting  
  - Model Regularization Techniques (Dropout, L1/L2 Regularization)  
- **Practice**: Tune models using cross-validation and grid search  
- **Applications**: Improving model performance through optimal parameter settings  

---

### 7. Natural Language Processing (Weeks 13-14)  
- **Topics**:  
  - Text Preprocessing (Tokenization, Lemmatization, Stemming)  
  - Bag of Words and TF-IDF  
  - Word Embeddings (Word2Vec, GloVe)  
  - Sequence Models (LSTMs, GRUs)  
  - Text Classification and Sentiment Analysis  
- **Practice**: Implement text classification models for NLP tasks  
- **Applications**: Sentiment analysis, text summarization  

---

### 8. Reinforcement Learning (Weeks 15-16)  
- **Topics**:  
  - Basics of Reinforcement Learning (Agents, Actions, Rewards)  
  - Markov Decision Processes  
  - Q-Learning  
  - Deep Q Networks (DQN)  
  - Policy Gradient Methods  
- **Practice**: Implement simple reinforcement learning agents  
- **Applications**: Game playing, robotic control, optimization problems  

---

### 9. Dimensionality Reduction (Weeks 17-18)  
- **Topics**:  
  - Principal Component Analysis (PCA)  
  - Singular Value Decomposition (SVD)  
  - t-SNE and UMAP for Nonlinear Dimensionality Reduction  
- **Practice**: Apply dimensionality reduction techniques on high-dimensional datasets  
- **Applications**: Feature engineering, visualization of complex datasets  

---

### 10. Model Deployment (Weeks 19-20)  
- **Topics**:  
  - Introduction to Model Deployment  
  - Deployment with Flask and FastAPI  
  - Using Cloud Platforms (AWS, Google Cloud, Azure) for ML Model Deployment  
  - Monitoring and Updating Deployed Models  
- **Practice**: Deploy machine learning models to production environments  
- **Applications**: Making machine learning models available for real-world use-e 

---
**Up to:** [[Index]]

# Introduction

**Date:** 2026-05-20  
**Subject:** Physics  
**Parent MOC:** [[TODO_LINK_THIS]]  
**Topic:** {{topic}}  
**Source / Reference:**  

---

## Learning Objectives  
-  

---

## Key Concepts and Laws  
- Physics is regarded as an experimental science
- Physicists observe natural phenomena and their effects and explain them using theories
- More well and widely used theories are called laws or principles
- A theory is based on accepted fundamental principles and observations, it does not imply it as unproven concept or a random thought
- Physical Theory undertakes the following steps:
	- Ask appropriate questions
	- Design experiments to answer those questions
	- Draw conclusions from the results of the experiment(s)
- No theory is regarded as the final or ultimate truth, you cannot "prove" that is always correct, you can only find situations where the said theory does not ideally hold and adjust it to fit those parameters or discard it entirely. The conditions for which the theory is said to be true is called the range of validity.
- ### Idealized Model
	- This is a simplified version of a subject which a theory is to be applied upon, like assuming a ball to be a point mass etc.
	- The goal of this is to give a general but relatively usable value from what we're solving, if we put in too much details, it soon looses it's everyday use.
	- We keep the important parts and assume the rest to be negligible
	- Validity of the predictions made is limited to the validity of the model
- ### Standards and Units
	- Physical quantities are numbers used to describe physical phenomena quantitatively
	- The explanation of a measure of a physical quantity is called an **operational definition**
	- Physical quantities are described by how we calculate from other fundamental quantities
	- Measuring a quantity references it to a given **standard** whose unary value is a **unit**. meter is the unit of distance, second is the unit of time
	- When using a number to represent a physical quantity or phenomena, always attach it's valid unit
	- Unit prefixes, kinda like size modifiers: $k, n, G, m$ etc
- ### Unit Consistency and Conversions
	- Equations must always be dimensionally consistent meaning units on one side must equal units on the other side. You can't add apples and oranges so adding a second to a kilogram wouldn't make sense, multiplication and division of units however may hold
	- Units should be treated just like algebraic symbols in equations in terms of operations and handling
	- Always include units in calculations
	- You can use **unit multipliers** for conversions (check textbook)

FILE: ./07_Physics/07_Physics.md
════════════════════════════════════════
# Physics 

### Key Topics and Notes
- [Classical_Mechanics MOC](./00_MOCs/01_Classical_Mechanics_MOC.md)
- [Thermodynamics_and_Statistical_Physics MOC](./00_MOCs/02_Thermodynamics_and_Statistical_Physics_MOC.md)
- [Computational_Methods_in_Physics MOC](./00_MOCs/03_Computational_Methods_in_Physics_MOC.md)
- [Waves_and_Oscillations MOC](./00_MOCs/04_Waves_and_Oscillations_MOC.md)
- [Electromagnetism MOC](./00_MOCs/05_Electromagnetism_MOC.md)
- [Optics MOC](./00_MOCs/06_Optics_MOC.md)
- [Quantum_Mechanics MOC](./00_MOCs/07_Quantum_Mechanics_MOC.md)
- [Special_Relativity MOC](./00_MOCs/08_Special_Relativity_MOC.md)
- [Solid-State_Physics MOC](./00_MOCs/09_Solid-State_Physics_MOC.md)

## Resources  
- [University Physics by Young and Freedman](./01_Resources/01_University_Physics_Young.pdf)
- [Fundamentals of Physics by Resnick, Halliday, and Walker](./01_Resources/02_Fundamentals_Of_Physics_David.pdf) 
- [Electrodynamics by David J. Griffiths](./01_Resources/03_Intro_to_Electrodynamics_Griffith.pdf)  
- [Computational Physics by Mark Newman](./01_Resources/04_Computational_Physics_Newman.pdf)  
- [Practice Problems](./02_Practice-Problems.md)  
- [MIT OpenCourseWare: Physics Resources](https://ocw.mit.edu/)  
- **Toolkits**: Python (NumPy/SciPy, Matplotlib), MATLAB, Wolfram Alpha, or any preferred simulation software  

## Progress  
### Foundations  
- [ ] Classical Mechanics  
- [ ] Thermodynamics and Statistical Physics  
- [ ] Computational Methods in Physics  

### Core Concepts  
- [ ] Waves and Oscillations  
- [ ] Electromagnetism  
- [ ] Optics  

### Advanced Topics  
- [ ] Quantum Mechanics  
- [ ] Special Relativity  
- [ ] Solid-State Physics  

---

## Syllabus  

### 1. Classical Mechanics (Weeks 1-4)  
- **Topics**:  
  - Kinematics and Dynamics of Motion  
  - Newton's Laws of Motion  
  - Work, Energy, and Power  
  - Momentum and Collisions  
  - Rotational Dynamics and Angular Momentum  
  - Oscillatory Motion  
- **Practice**: Solve problems related to motion, energy conservation, and rotational dynamics  
- **Applications**: Robotics, simulation of physical systems  

---

### 2. Thermodynamics and Statistical Physics (Weeks 5-6)  
- **Topics**:  
  - Laws of Thermodynamics  
  - Heat and Work  
  - Entropy and the Carnot Cycle  
  - Statistical Interpretation of Entropy  
  - Kinetic Theory of Gases  
- **Practice**: Analyze heat engines, solve problems involving entropy and statistical distributions  
- **Applications**: Energy systems, thermal management in electronics  

---

### 3. Computational Methods in Physics (Weeks 7-10)  
- **Topics**:  
  - Numerical Solutions to Differential Equations  
  - Monte Carlo Methods  
  - Simulation of Physical Systems (Mechanics, Electrodynamics)  
  - Data Analysis and Visualization  
  - Fourier Transform Applications in Physics  
  - Optimization Techniques in Physics Simulations  
- **Practice**:  
  - Write Python programs for simulations (mechanics, electromagnetism).  
  - Create visualizations using Matplotlib.  
  - Solve boundary conditions using numerical methods.  
- **Applications**:  
  - Scientific computing, control systems.  
  - Computational electromagnetics, ray-tracing.  
  - Data-driven analysis of physical models.  

---

### 4. Waves and Oscillations (Weeks 11-12)  
- **Topics**:  
  - Mechanical and Electromagnetic Waves  
  - Wave Equation and Speed of Waves  
  - Superposition and Interference  
  - Standing Waves and Resonance  
  - Fourier Analysis of Waves  
- **Practice**: Analyze wave phenomena in mechanical and electromagnetic systems  
- **Applications**: Signal processing, acoustics  

---

### 5. Electromagnetism (Weeks 13-17)  
- **Topics**:  
  - Electrostatics and Coulomb's Law  
  - Electric Potential and Capacitance  
  - Magnetic Fields and Ampère’s Law  
  - Electromagnetic Induction and Faraday's Law  
  - Maxwell's Equations  
  - Electromagnetic Waves  
- **Practice**: Solve problems using Maxwell's equations, analyze circuits and field interactions  
- **Applications**: Microprocessor functioning, electromagnetic shielding in devices, antenna design, and circuit optimization  

---

### 6. Optics (Weeks 18-19)  
- **Topics**:  
  - Reflection and Refraction  
  - Lenses and Mirrors  
  - Wave Optics: Interference, Diffraction, and Polarization  
  - Fiber Optics and Applications  
- **Practice**: Analyze optical systems using ray tracing and wave equations  
- **Applications**: Optical communication, imaging technologies  

---

### 7. Quantum Mechanics (Weeks 20-23)  
- **Topics**:  
  - Wave-Particle Duality  
  - Schrödinger Equation and Applications  
  - Quantum States and Operators  
  - Atomic and Molecular Structure  
  - Quantum Tunneling and Applications  
- **Practice**: Solve problems involving quantum behavior of particles  
- **Applications**: Quantum computing, semiconductor design  

---

### 8. Special Relativity (Weeks 24-25)  
- **Topics**:  
  - Postulates of Special Relativity  
  - Time Dilation and Length Contraction  
  - Relativistic Energy and Momentum  
  - Lorentz Transformations  
  - Applications in High-Energy Physics  
- **Practice**: Analyze relativistic effects in moving systems  
- **Applications**: Particle accelerators, GPS systems  

---

### 9. Solid-State Physics (Weeks 26-28)  
- **Topics**:  
  - Crystal Lattice and Unit Cells  
  - Band Theory of Solids  
  - Electrical and Thermal Properties of Solids  
  - Semiconductors, Transistors, and IC Design  
- **Practice**: Analyze semiconductor behavior and solid-state devices  
- **Applications**: Electronics, material science  

---

### Bonus: Physics of Computation (Optional Week 29)  
- **Topics**:  
  - Thermodynamics of Information  
  - Landauer's Principle  
  - Physical Limits of Computation  
  - Reversible Computing  
- **Applications**: Understanding computational limits in hardware and energy systems-e 

---
**Up to:** [[Index]]

## Resources  
- [Genki I](./01_Resources/01_Genki_1_Eri.pdf)
- [Genki II](./01_Resources/02_Genki_2_Eri.pdf)
- [Tae Kim’s Guide to Japanese Grammar](./03_A_Guide_to_Japanese_Grammar_Tae_Kim)  
- Bunpro.jp (Grammar practice with SRS)  
- Jisho.org (Online Japanese dictionary)  
- NHK World Easy Japanese lessons   
- Tobira: Gateway to Advanced Japanese  

## Progress  
### Introduction & Writing System  
- [ ] Introduction  
- [ ] Hiragana  
- [ ] Katakana  
- [ ] Kanji  

### Basic Grammar  
- [ ] Expressing State-of-Being  
- [ ] Introduction to Particles （は、も、が）  
- [ ] Adjectives  
- [ ] Verb Basics  
- [ ] Negative Verbs  
- [ ] Past Tense  
- [ ] Particles used with verbs （を、に、へ、で）  
- [ ] Transitive and Intransitive Verbs  
- [ ] Descriptive Relative Clauses and Sentence Order  
- [ ] Noun-related Particles （と、や、とか、の）  
- [ ] Adverbs and Sentence-ending particles  

### Essential Grammar  
- [ ] Polite Form and Verb Stems （～です、～ます）  
- [ ] Addressing People  
- [ ] The Question Marker （か）  
- [ ] Compound Sentences （て-form、から、ので、のに、が、けど、し、～たりする）  
- [ ] Other uses of the te-form （～ている、～てある、～ておく、～ていく、～てくる）  
- [ ] Potential Form  
- [ ] Using する and なる with the に particle （～[よう]になる／する）  
- [ ] Conditionals （と、なら、ば、たら）  
- [ ] Expressing "must" or "have to" （～だめ、～いけない、～ならない、～ても）  
- [ ] Desire and Suggestions （たい、欲しい、volitional、～たらどう）  
- [ ] Performing an action on a relative clause （と、って）  
- [ ] Defining and Describing （という）  
- [ ] Trying something out or attempting to do something （～てみる、volitional+とする）  
- [ ] Giving and Receiving （あげる、やる、くれる、もらう）  
- [ ] Making requests （～ください、～ちょうだい、～なさい、command form）  

### Numbers, Casual Speech & Special Expressions  
- [ ] Numbers and Counting  
- [ ] Casual Patterns and Slang  
- [ ] Review and more sentence-ending particles  
- [ ] Causative and Passive Verbs  
- [ ] Honorific and Humble Forms  
- [ ] Things that happen unintentionally （～てしまう、～ちゃう／～じゃう）  
- [ ] Special expressions with generic nouns （こと、ところ、もの）  
- [ ] Expressing various levels of certainty （かもしれない、でしょう、だろう）  
- [ ] Expressing amounts （だけ、のみ、しか、ばかり、すぎる、Amount+も、ほど、さ）  
- [ ] Various ways to express similarity and hearsay （よう、～みたい、～そう、～そうだ、～らしい、～っぽい）  
- [ ] Using 方 and よる for comparisons and other functions （より、の方、stem＋方、によって、によると）  
- [ ] Saying something is easy or difficult to do （～やすい、～にくい）  
- [ ] More negative verbs （ないで、ず、～ん、ぬ）  
- [ ] Hypothesizing and Concluding （わけ、～とする）  
- [ ] Expressing time-specific actions （ばかり、とたんに、ながら、まくる）  
- [ ] Leaving something the way it is （まま、っぱなし）  

### Advanced Topics  
- [ ] Formal Expressions （である、ではない）  
- [ ] Things that should be a certain way （はず、べき、べく、べからず）  
- [ ] Expressing the minimum expectation （でさえ、ですら、おろか）  
- [ ] Showing signs of something （～がる、ばかり、～めく）  
- [ ] Formal expressions of non-feasibility （～ざるを得ない、やむを得ない、～かねる）  
- [ ] Tendencies （～がち、～つつ、きらいがある）  
- [ ] Advanced Volitional （まい、であろう、かろう）  
- [ ] Covered by something （だらけ、まみれ、ずくめ）  
- [ ] Advanced proximity of actions （が早いか、や否や、そばから）  
- [ ] Others （思いきや、がてら、あげく）  

---

## Syllabus  

### 1. Introduction & Writing System (Weeks 1–2)  
- **Topics**:  
  - Overview of Japanese language  
  - Hiragana chart and usage  
  - Katakana chart and usage  
  - Introduction to Kanji  
- **Practice**:  
  - Writing and reading hiragana and katakana  
  - Basic Kanji recognition  
- **Applications**:  
  - Reading simple Japanese texts  
  - Writing basic words and greetings  

---

### 2. Basic Grammar (Weeks 3–6)  
- **Topics**:  
  - Expressing state-of-being  
  - Particles は、も、が  
  - Adjectives and their forms  
  - Verb basics and conjugations  
  - Negative verbs and past tense  
  - Verb-related particles を、に、へ、で  
  - Transitive vs intransitive verbs  
  - Relative clauses and sentence order  
  - Noun-related particles と、や、とか、の  
  - Adverbs and sentence-ending particles  
- **Practice**:  
  - Constructing simple sentences  
  - Particle usage drills  
  - Verb conjugation exercises  
- **Applications**:  
  - Everyday conversation  
  - Describing people, places, and actions  

---

### 3. Essential Grammar (Weeks 7–12)  
- **Topics**:  
  - Polite forms ～です、～ます  
  - Addressing people and question marker か  
  - Compound sentences with て-form and conjunctions  
  - Uses of the te-form  
  - Potential form and conditionals  
  - Expressing necessity and obligation  
  - Desire and suggestions  
  - Giving and receiving expressions  
  - Making requests and commands  
- **Practice**:  
  - Dialogue practice  
  - Writing compound sentences  
  - Role-playing polite requests  
- **Applications**:  
  - Formal and informal conversations  
  - Expressing intentions and requests  

---

### 4. Numbers, Casual Speech & Special Expressions (Weeks 13–16)  
- **Topics**:  
  - Numbers and counting systems  
  - Casual speech patterns and slang  
  - Sentence-ending particles review  
  - Causative and passive forms  
  - Honorific and humble language  
  - Expressing unintentional actions  
  - Certainty, amounts, similarity, and hearsay expressions  
  - Comparison structures and ease/difficulty expressions  
- **Practice**:  
  - Listening to casual dialogues  
  - Practicing honorific and humble forms  
  - Using special expressions in sentences  
- **Applications**:  
  - Social conversations  
  - Understanding spoken Japanese in informal settings  

---

### 5. Advanced Topics (Weeks 17–20)  
- **Topics**:  
  - Formal expressions and expectations  
  - Signs and tendencies in language  
  - Advanced volitional forms  
  - Expressions of proximity and timing  
  - Miscellaneous advanced grammar points  
- **Practice**:  
  - Reading formal texts  
  - Writing essays using advanced grammar  
- **Applications**:  
  - Academic and professional Japanese  
  - Advanced reading comprehension  

---
-e 

---

# Art and Animation 

### Key Topics and Notes
- [Drawing_Basics_and_Perspective MOC](./00_MOCs/01_Drawing_Basics_and_Perspective_MOC.md)
- [Anatomy_and_Construction MOC](./00_MOCs/02_Anatomy_and_Construction_MOC.md)
- [Gesture_and_Flow_Sakuga MOC](./00_MOCs/03_Gesture_and_Flow_Sakuga_MOC.md)
- [Color_Theory_and_Light MOC](./00_MOCs/04_Color_Theory_and_Light_MOC.md)
- [Animation_Principles_2D_and_3D MOC](./00_MOCs/05_Animation_Principles_2D_and_3D_MOC.md)
- [3D_Modeling_and_Topology MOC](./00_MOCs/06_3D_Modeling_and_Topology_MOC.md)
- [Texturing_and_Shading MOC](./00_MOCs/07_Texturing_and_Shading_MOC.md)
- [Rigging_and_Weight_Painting MOC](./00_MOCs/08_Rigging_and_Weight_Painting_MOC.md)
- [FX_Animation_and_Physics MOC](./00_MOCs/09_FX_Animation_and_Physics_MOC.md)
- [Blender_Python_and_Pipeline_Tools MOC](./00_MOCs/10_Blender_Python_and_Pipeline_Tools_MOC.md)

## Resources
- [Ebook: Fun With a Pencil, Andrew Loomis](./01_Resources/01_Fun_With_a_Pencil_Loomis.pdf)
- [Ebook: Figure Drawing: Design and Invention, Michael Hampton](./01_Resources/02_Figure_Drawing_Design_and_Invention_Hampton.pdf)
- [Ebook: FORCE: Dynamic Life Drawing for Animators, Mike Mattesi](./01_Resources/03_FORCE_Dynamic_Life_Drawing_Mattesi.pdf)
- [Ebook: The Animator's Survival Kit, Richard Williams](./01_Resources/04_Animators_Survival_Kit_Williams.pdf)
- [Ebook: The Pushing Points Topology Workbook, William Vaughan](./01_Resources/05_Pushing_Points_Topology_Vaughan.pdf)
- [Ebook: Color and Light, James Gurney](./01_Resources/06_Color_and_Light_Gurney.pdf)
- [Ebook: Elemental Magic, Joseph Gilland](./01_Resources/07_Elemental_Magic_Gilland.pdf)
- [Blender Documentation](https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/)

## Progress
### Fundamentals (2D)
- [ ] Drawing Basics and Perspective
- [ ] Anatomy and Construction
- [ ] Gesture and Flow (Sakuga)
- [ ] Color Theory and Light

### Core Animation
- [ ] Animation Principles (12 Principles)
- [ ] Keyframing and Timing
- [ ] FX Animation and Physics

### 3D Pipeline (Blender)
- [ ] 3D Modeling and Topology
- [ ] Texturing and Shading
- [ ] Rigging and Weight Painting
- [ ] Integration (Grease Pencil/Compositing)

## Syllabus

### 1. The Absolute Beginning: Form & Perspective (Weeks 1-4)
- **Topics**:
  - Pencil control and line confidence (Drawabox basics)
  - Construction: Spheres, Cubes, Cylinders in perspective
  - The "Mannequin" head and basic face proportions
- **Practice**: Complete the first half of *Fun With a Pencil* (Loomis); Draw 50 boxes in 3-point perspective
- **Applications**: Understanding 3D space on a 2D plane (essential for both Krita and Blender viewport navigation)

### 2. Anatomy and Construction (Weeks 5-12)
- **Topics**:
  - The body as a machine: Interlocking shapes
  - Major muscle groups (Torso, Arms, Legs)
  - Hands and Feet mechanics
  - Volume preservation in rotation
- **Practice**: Study *Figure Drawing: Design and Invention* (Hampton); daily life drawing sessions (1 hr)
- **Applications**: Character design, organic modeling preparation

### 3. The Sakuga Engine: Gesture and Flow (Weeks 13-20)
- **Topics**:
  - Rhythm, Line of Action, and Force curves
  - Drawing "Verbs" vs "Nouns"
  - Exaggeration and Dynamic Posing
  - Silhouette clarity
- **Practice**: *FORCE* (Mattesi) exercises; 30-second gesture drawings; Keyframe posing in Krita
- **Applications**: Rigging deformation, creating dynamic animation keyframes

### 4. Making it Move: Animation Principles (Weeks 21-28)
- **Topics**:
  - Timing and Spacing (Charts)
  - Squash and Stretch
  - Anticipation, Overshoot, and Settle
  - Walk Cycles (2D and 3D)
- **Practice**: *The Animator's Survival Kit* exercises; Bouncing ball, Flour Sack animation
- **Applications**: 2D Anime (Sakuga) and 3D Character Animation

### 5. The 3D Bridge: Topology and Modeling (Weeks 29-36)
- **Topics**:
  - Hard Surface vs. Organic Modeling
  - Edge Loops, Poles, and Quads
  - Retopology for Animation
  - UV Unwrapping logic
- **Practice**: *Topology Workbook* (Vaughan); Model a character bust with clean topology in Blender
- **Applications**: Creating game-ready or film-ready assets that deform correctly

### 6. Texturing, Lighting, and Rendering (Weeks 37-44)
- **Topics**:
  - Light Physics: Diffuse, Specular, Ambient Occlusion
  - Color Scripts and Mood
  - PBR (Physically Based Rendering) vs. NPR (Non-Photorealistic/Anime Rendering)
  - Shader Nodes in Blender
- **Practice**: *Color and Light* (Gurney) studies; Texture the character bust using Krita and Blender
- **Applications**: Visual development, final frame rendering

### 7. Advanced Pipeline: Rigging and FX (Weeks 45-52)
- **Topics**:
  - Bones, Armatures, and Weight Painting
  - Forward Kinematics (FK) vs Inverse Kinematics (IK)
  - Elemental FX (Fire, Water, Smoke) principles
  - Blender Grease Pencil integration
- **Practice**: *Elemental Magic* studies; Rig the character; Animate a short combat sequence with FX
- **Applications**: Full production pipeline, "Sakuga" style finished shots
-e
